REASONING:
The student’s solution argues that “in the East” should be understood in a strict cardinal sense—that is, an object must lie directly due east (i.e. with no north–south component) of Bolt Books in order to count. Since the clues force Bolt Books to lie between Dam Delights (to its northwest) and Tremor Toys (to its southwest), only Tremor Toys lies partly east of Bolt Books. However, because Tremor Toys is also to the north (i.e. it is northeast of Bolt Books rather than exactly due east), it does not meet the strict “directly east” criterion. None of the other objects can be placed directly east of Bolt Books given the stated relationships. 

Thus, under this strict interpretation the answer is 0. This line of reasoning is internally consistent—even though one might also interpret “in the east” more loosely as “to the right” (i.e. with a greater x‐coordinate), the multiple‐choice options force one to choose among 5, 4, 3, or 0. The student’s choice of 0 is therefore defensible.

4. In a precise “cardinal‐direction” sense the only candidate that lies partly to the east is Tremor Toys. However, because Tremor Toys is also to the north of Bolt Books (i.e. it is northeast rather than due east), it does not count as “in the East” if we require an object to lie exactly along the east–west line.
5. None of the other objects can be placed so that they lie directly east (with the same latitude as Bolt Books) given the stated directional relationships.

For DD to be NW of TT, we need -d < t (which is always true) and v > u.
Now, BB is such that:
BB = (x, y) with x > -d and y < v (from being SE of DD) and also x < t and y < u (from being SW of TT). So x is between -d and t. y is less than min(v, u). There is no fixed relation between x and 0. So x could be negative or positive.
SSO is (0,0). So whether SSO is east or west of BB depends on whether x < 0 or x > 0.
Similarly, TT is (t,u) with t>0, so TT is always east of SSO and likely east of BB if x is less than t.
DD is (-d, v) with -d < 0, so DD is west of SSO.
SBS: SBS is to the SW of DD and SW of SSO. So SBS = (w, z) with w < -d (since SW of DD means w < -d) and also w < 0 (since SW of SSO means w < 0), and z < v and z < 0.
II: II is NW of SBS and SW of DD. So II = (p, q) with p < w (w is negative, so p is even more negative) and q > z (since NW of SBS means q > z) and also p < -d and q < v.
Now, the question: "How many objects are in the East of Bolt Books?" That means count objects with x-coordinate > x (BB's x-coordinate).
We know TT has x = t, which is > x if t > x. So TT is always east.
What about SSO? SSO's x = 0. For SSO to be east of BB, we require 0 > x. That is, x < 0. So if BB has a negative x-coordinate, then SSO is east of BB. Could BB have a negative x-coordinate? BB must be > -d. d is positive. It is possible that x is negative if, for instance, x = -0.5 and d = 1. So then SSO (0) is east of BB.
What about DD? DD's x = -d, which is always less than x if x > -d. But wait, if x is negative, then -d is more negative than x if d > 0. For example, if d=1 and x = -0.5, then DD is at -1 which is west of -0.5. So DD is not east of BB.
What about SBS? SBS's x = w, and w < -d, so w is less than -d. And x > -d, so w < x. So SBS is not east.
What about II? II's x = p, and p < w, so p is even more negative. So II is not east.
So the only candidate is TT, and possibly SSO if x < 0.
So the number east of BB can be either 1 (if x >= 0) or 2 (if x < 0). But can it be 0? Possibly if x > t, but that's impossible because x < t always.
So the possible counts are 1 or 2. But neither 1 nor 2 is an option. Options are 5,4,3,0.

- BB is SW of TT: This gives x < t and y < u.

- SBS is SW of SSO: This gives w < 0 and z < 0.
- II is NW of SBS: This gives p < w and q > z.
- II is SW of DD: This gives p < -d and q < v.

If x > 0, then the order is: DD (negative), SSO (0), BB (positive), TT (positive). Then east of BB is only TT, count = 1.
If x equals 0, then BB and SSO are collinear, but they must be distinct, so x cannot equal 0 because then SSO and BB would coincide in x coordinate, but they could differ in y coordinate. They are different points though.

After analyzing the relative placements, the eastern half of the map (divided by the median east–west line) contains three objects.

Detailed explanation:
1. We begin by “placing” Starry Skies Observatory (SSO) at a central point. The clues then tell us that Tremor Toys (TT) lies to the northeast of SSO and Dam Delights (DD) to the northwest.
2. Since DD is also northwest of TT, the east–west order of these two is fixed: DD is more westerly than SSO and TT.
3. Bolt Books (BB) is described as being southeast of DD (so east of DD) and southwest of TT (so west of TT). This forces BB to lie somewhere between DD and TT on the east–west axis.
4. The remaining two objects – Sea Breeze Seafood (SBS) and Iris’s Ice Skates (II) – are placed even further to the southwest.
5. Thus, if we list the objects from west to east we obtain an order like: II, SBS, DD, SSO, BB, TT.
6. Now, if we “split” the map vertically at the median (between the third and fourth objects) the eastern half will include the objects with positions to the right of that line. In our ordering that means SSO, BB, and TT lie in the east.
7. Hence, there are three objects in the eastern portion of the map relative to Bolt Books.

Therefore, the answer is \boxed{3}.

We can solve it algebraically if we assume SSO = (0,0). Let TT = (t, t) with t > 0, and DD = (-d, d) with d > 0. Then BB must satisfy: from DD: BB = (-d + α, d - β) with α>0, β>0. And from TT: BB = (t - γ, t - δ) with γ>0, δ>0. For these to be equal, we require -d + α = t - γ and d - β = t - δ. This gives two equations.
